**Body-side jobs** (wiring in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#body-side-wiring)):

- **Sport button (optional).** It switches the DME's throttle map. Fit the M3's console switch unit (61316925520 on coupes, 61316925521 on convertibles) and wire its plug X1869: pin 17 to X60004 pin 15 for the button, pin 18 to X60004 pin 16 for its LED. One non-M swap did exactly that, and it worked ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/e46-m3-sport-button-retrofit-on-non-m.1286041/)). A base Touring with only a DSC button also lacks the switch center's terminal-30 and terminal-R feeds and its K-bus wire, on X1869 pins 7, 22 and 23 ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#sport-button-and-switch-center), [M346 Projects – E46 M3 SMG Touring build, part 2 (2023), 20:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=VHCrVhVuFzY&t=1245s)). **Watch:** [Tenza Motorsports – 5 things they don't tell you, part 2 (2021), 08:45](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mDBiRn30fEs&t=525s).
- **Cruise control** needs the brake-switch move, the clutch chain through X6011 pin 7 and the gear switch to X60002 pin 20, and the M3's clutch-switch module, 61319122700. The module is required, not optional: the M3's sends +12 V with the pedal up, and your Touring's (61319231129) doesn't ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/225235-clutch-switch-help-code-50)). Two S54 builds cured no cruise with it ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-cluster-swap-dsc-light.1260275/), [NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/member-build-journals/3768-what-s-going-on-in-my-shop-currently/page3)). The 420G's gear switch is 23142229113 ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/23142229113/)).
- **Keep the start interlock.** The clutch module's fourth wire to EWS pin 8, fitted to US and Japanese cars, stays as it is ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-manual-swap-clutch-pedal-sensor-wiring.1316805/)).
- **Sensor leads that may not reach** the M3's parts: the coolant-level sensor (the M3 expansion tank sits on the other side), the washer pump and level sensor (the M3 tank sits low), and the radiator-outlet sensor ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swap-wiring-information.1003871/), [Tracks and Trails – Attempting to drive my E46 M3 Touring (2026), 22:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pvR3ovRfQHg&t=1360s)).
- **Heater valve.** The US M3's valve carries a small electric pump that your Touring's plain valve lacks. Either fit your plain valve (64118369805) on the S54, with no wiring or coding, or keep the M3 valve and wire and code its pump. One builder who left the pump unwired found his cooling system hard to bleed ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#heater-valve-and-auxiliary-water-pump), [UNRIVALED – Watch this before you S54 swap (2021), 02:32](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eddLff8yba0&t=152s)). See [Cooling and oil cooler](#cooling-and-oil-cooler).
- **A/C.** Both climate controls send the A/C request over the K-bus to the cluster, which passes it to the DME over CAN. A/C with the S54 is confirmed on cars with automatic climate control (IHKA). Nobody has reported first-hand on manual climate control (IHKR), but BMW describes the same messages, so it should work; test it early ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#ac-with-manual-or-automatic-climate-control), [e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/kalims-s54-sedan-swap-warning-pic-heavy.889867/page-38)).

![Re-pin map](diagrams/10-repin-map.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 10. The body-side re-pins on an MS43 Touring built 09/2001 to 02/2003 and an MS45.1 Touring built from 03/2003, each with an M3 donor built from 09/2001, drawn from where each wire is now (left) to where it goes (right) and numbered as in the manual's tables. Green marks a new wire, a dashed line an optional wire or one that may not be there, and an X a wire that comes out and is insulated. On an MS43 the heater wire leaves X6011 pin 7 before the clutch wire goes in; on an MS45.1 you tag pins 2 and 15, and the wires in pins 5 and 15 (and 19, if fitted) come out first. On both, every wire that crosses between X6011 and X60004 gets a new terminal of the target's type, and a recommended ground goes into X60004 pin 5.*

### Fuel-pump control

Your Touring and a US M3 switch their fuel pumps differently ([WDS](https://wds.psjr.org/en/e46/svg/SP0000014336.svg), [WDS](https://wds.psjr.org/en/e46/svg/SP0000014408.svg), [BMW ST041](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST041%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle/3%20EngineElectronics%20%28Part%202%29.pdf), [ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Touring/USA/325i-M54/L-N/browse/vehicle_electrical_system/relay_for_fuel_pump_1_k96/)):

| Car | What switches the pump | DME output |
|---|---|---|
| Your 325i Touring (M54, MS43 or MS45.1) | Relay K96 (white-green, 61368373700) behind the glovebox. The DME grounds its coil through a black/violet (SW/VI) wire | X60004 pin 10 |
| Euro M3 | Relay K96 | X60004 pin 10 |
| US M3 | A pump module (EKP, 61357892976) that slows the pump at idle and part load, in a coupe's trunk above the right rear wheel or behind a convertible's right rear side trim ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/fuel-pump-relay-location-left-me-stranded.1316926/)) | X60002 pin 11, a 0–120 Hz PWM signal, out through the E-box plug X6021 pin 1 |

On the US M3's drawings X60004 pin 10 is "not used", a Euro-only output. Builders report that US software drives it anyway: swap shops run K96 from it ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swap-california-bar-certification.1307461/page-2), [e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/kalims-s54-sedan-swap-warning-pic-heavy.889867/page-38)). BMW's drawings put K96 on pin 10 on both of your DMEs, the MS45.1 too; the workbook tab that lists pin 10 as unused is the M56 layout ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#how-each-car-switches-the-pump)).

**Recommended: leave your pump wiring as it is.** The MSS54's pin-10 output then switches your K96 relay. A swap shop that does this on non-M E46s: "I don't modify anything in the fuel pump wiring system, this DOES leave a shadow code for the Fuel Pump Relay (shows in ISTA/INPA but not on a generic scan tool) but does not set the CEL", and the car "will pass an OBD2 plug in emissions test" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swap-california-bar-certification.1307461/page-2)). Kassel's swap chart lists no pump changes for any E46 ([Kassel](https://www.kasselperformance.com/wp-content/uploads/2016/01/E46S54Wiring.png)). Three checks, all part of the manual's [checks before first power-up](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#checks-on-the-pump-circuit):

1. **Pin 10 reaches K96's coil.** Continuity from the X60004 pin-10 cavity to pin 4 of the relay's socket. Then, at key-on, the pump primes once, and Tool32's `STEUERN_EKP` makes K96 click; the click proves your software drives pin 10.
2. **The PWM wire stays off the relay.** On a manual 325i the body side of X6021 pin 1 is empty, so the M3's pump-module wire connects to nothing ([WDS](https://wds.psjr.org/en/e46/svg/SP0000007729.svg)). Keep it that way. A relay coil can't follow a 0–120 Hz signal and would likely chatter; nobody has reported trying it ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/132772-help-fuel-pump-relay-ekp-engine-wiring-schematic/page2)).
3. **Expect the stored fault:** location 1 (0x01), "Relais Kraftstoffpumpe", because US software misses its pump module. It shows in INPA, ISTA and PA-Soft, not on a generic scanner, and doesn't light the check-engine lamp; INPA prints it as 1. Don't confuse it with 130 (0x82), the immobilizer fault. A real pump fault carries the same number, so tell them apart by the car: with one prime at key-on, 5.0 bar and a running engine it's the expected fault; with no prime and no pressure it's real ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#faults-that-look-like-pump-faults)).

**If your state reads manufacturer fault codes,** or you want a clean fault memory, you have two alternatives:

- **Fit the M3's EKP module.** It takes five wires where the relay takes four, and its signal needs a terminal and a new wire in your side of X6021 pin 1 ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#optional-the-m3-pump-module-ekp)). Kalim suggests rewiring the socket behind the glovebox for it ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/kalims-s54-sedan-swap-warning-pic-heavy.889867/page-38)). It brings back the slow-running pump and BMW's documented crash cut-off. Nobody has documented it in a Touring.
- **Have the DME vendor suppress the fault.** One swapper planned to have it "coded out" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/kalims-s54-sedan-swap-warning-pic-heavy.889867/page-38)), and Kassel's "Engine Swap Coding" (\$349) includes "any and all options" for a swap ([Kassel](https://www.kasselperformance.com/product/bmw-e46-m3-s54-mss54-hp-tuning/)); ask before you order.

Don't flash Euro DME software to get rid of the fault. It drops the functions the pin tables mark "USA only", including the DM-TL leak test you need for inspection (our reading of the [pinout sheet](https://docs.google.com/spreadsheets/d/1I_2XAvziPCiRlZXnkfk4x1tUIKy4TKPbmdJcGCBdGIg/edit?usp=sharing)).

With the relay, your pump runs at full speed whenever the engine runs. That sends more fuel back to the tank's jet pump and makes more pump heat and noise; no failures have been reported from it. An E46 has no wire from the airbag unit to the pump: the airbag unit sends a crash message on the K-bus, the cluster passes it on over CAN, and the DME switches the pump off ([BMW ST034](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST034%20E46%20Complete%20Vehicle/7%20Passive%20Safety%20Systems.pdf)). The MSS54 has the function, but whether US software also drops its pin-10 relay output after a crash isn't documented ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#crash-fuel-cut-off)). All of this assumes an M54 Touring with this relay. The M56 SULEV engine has its own pump controller and a returnless metal tank, and a manual gearbox doesn't rule it out, so check your engine code ([BMW B11 75 02](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST055%20Engine%20Electronics%20%28Archive%202%29/08_SULEV.pdf), [Check your own Touring first](#check-your-own-touring-first)). The pressure and flow test, the M3 filter-regulator and the fuel lines are in [Fuel system](#fuel-system); the pins and checks are in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#fuel-pump-control).

![Fuel-pump control](diagrams/11-fuel-pump-control.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 11. The US MSS54 has two pump outputs. US wiring diagrams mark X60004 pin 10 "not used", but builders report that it still switches your K96 relay, so prove it on your car: one prime at key-on, and `STEUERN_EKP` makes K96 click. The X60002 pin-11 output, meant for the US M3's pump module, leaves the E-box at X6021 pin 1 and connects to nothing on a manual 325i, which stores fault location 1 (0x01) without lighting the check-engine lamp. Never connect that PWM output to the relay.*

### Immobilizer (EWS)

**How it works.** Your EWS reads the key's transponder through the ring antenna and checks the clutch input. It then releases the starter and sends a rolling code to the DME. If the code matches, the DME enables injection and ignition; if not, the engine cranks but won't start ([BMW ST051](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST051%20Body%20Electronics%20I%20%28Archive%201%29/05%20EWS.pdf)). The DME burns the code table into its memory the first time it pairs with an EWS: "once a DME is 'Married' to the vehicle it will not work in any other vehicle", and BMW's alignment job only resets the table to its first code (same source). So:

- **Cranks but won't start**, with fault 130 (0x82, "EWS manipulation") stored: the DME isn't accepting your EWS.
- **Won't crank:** the cause is upstream of the DME. Check the key, ring antenna, EWS, and the clutch input at EWS pin 8.
- **Starts, then dies:** not the immobilizer, which gives crank, no start. Look elsewhere, starting with the throttle system and the pump hold ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#wont-start)).

| Route | What it involves | Cost (October 2026) | In Tourings |
|---|---|---|---|
| **1. EWS delete (recommended)** | A vendor or a licensed tool sets the donor DME to skip the rolling-code check; your VIN goes in at the same time | \$100–\$300, plus \$20 for the VIN at Kassel | The usual route; your keys, locks and start interlock stay |
| 2. Virgin DME, synced | A vendor clears the DME's immobilizer memory, then you sync it to your EWS | About \$300 for the clearing, plus the risk that the sync fails | No documented success; two failures |
| 3. Donor EWS and keys | Fit the M3's EWS with its DME and use its key | Parts from the donor | Cleared the EWS fault in one 325iT, which still didn't start; you carry two keys |

**Route 1: the delete.** It's one setting in the DME's data, changed by the vendor or by a licensed tool ([ECUWorx](https://www.ecuworx.co.uk/how-to/a-run-down-of-features-of-the-mss5x-binary-modification-tool-and-what-they-do/), [Kassel](https://www.kasselperformance.com/product/bmw-e46-m3-s54-mss54-hp-tuning/)). Afterwards, "the EWS module is now simply looking for a proper matchup with the key transponder" ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/38046-mss54-ews-delete-anyone-successful)): your EWS still checks the key and the clutch and still controls the starter, and that's the only immobilizer layer left. Fault 130 (0x82) may stay stored unless the tampering fault is deleted too; one owner "confirmed that the engine can start" with it (same source).

| Vendor | Service and price (October 2026) |
|---|---|
| [Kassel Performance](https://www.kasselperformance.com/product/bmw-e46-m3-s54-mss54-hp-tuning/) | EWS delete \$109; VIN recode \$20; "Engine Swap Coding" with all swap options and a consult \$349; optional \$429 core charge |
| [DUDMD](https://www.dudmd.com/products/e46-m3-mss54-ews-delete-service) | Bench flash \$215; remote flash with a cable you keep \$225 (needs a Windows 8 or newer laptop) |
| [Severn Tuning](https://www.severntuning.com/products/bmw-mss54-hp-ews-delete-2001-2006-e46-m3) | MSS54 and MSS54HP delete, \$100, or \$200 with its "Shipping" option; listed "for off-road use only" |
| [RPM Motorsport](https://www.rpmmotorsport.net/collections/bmw-unlocked-ecu-service-ews-cas-delete/products/bmw-e46-m3-s54-m5-e39-s62-ews-delete-ready-to-start) | "Ready to start" delete, \$300, 1–2 business days after arrival |
| [Poverty Built](https://povertybuilt.com/products/e46-m3-s54-custom-tune-mss54) | Tunes from \$220, with immobilizer and swap configuration on request |

You can also read the DME yourself, have the file done remotely and flash it back, as Tracks and Trails did ([Tracks and Trails – Attempting to drive my E46 M3 Touring (2026), 1:12:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=pvR3ovRfQHg&t=4360s)), or run ECUWorx's licensed tool yourself ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#session-2-dme-vin-ews-delete-and-gearbox-software)). When you order:

1. Read and save the DME's identification, VIN slots and fault memory before you ship it.
2. Order the delete with your Touring's full 17-character VIN. If the donor was an SMG car, ask for manual-gearbox software too. SMG software left in place kept one swapped sedan in limp mode ([UNRIVALED – Watch this before you S54 swap (2021), 02:02](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=eddLff8yba0&t=122s)).
3. Decline the secondary-air, rear-O2, catalyst-efficiency and EVAP deletes on these vendors' menus. They're illegal on a road car.
4. When the DME comes back, read your VIN out of it.

> [!IMPORTANT]
> Keep the clutch-start interlock. Leave the clutch switch's wire to EWS pin 8 in place and the EWS parameter `PARK/NEUTRAL_EINGANG` at `aktiv` ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-how-to-code-ews-to-ignore-clutch-park-neutral-switch.1313143/)). A popular swap video recodes it to `nicht_aktiv` to get a car to start ([ShopLifeTV – Starting the S54 swap car for the first time (2015), 01:08](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=BV1yvGP7sCM&t=68s)), which lets the engine crank in gear. If the car won't crank, find the fault instead.

**Route 2: sync a virgin DME.** Only a DME whose immobilizer memory has been cleared can learn your EWS ([BMW ST051](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST051%20Body%20Electronics%20I%20%28Archive%201%29/05%20EWS.pdf)). That's a vendor job, done through the DME's programming port: RPM Motorsport offers used-DME EWS programming for \$300, and in October 2026 Kassel listed virginizing only for the M54's MS43 ([RPM](https://rpmmotorsport.net/products/bmw-e39-e46-m3-m5-s54-s62-used-ecu-dme-ews-programming), [Kassel](https://www.kasselperformance.com/product/ms43-virginize/)). The Tool32 sync sequence that circulates worked once, on one owner's CSL-clone MSS54: a DME converted in hardware to run CSL software, whose immobilizer code (ISN) he had written in through its BDM programming port. He wasn't sure it works on a virgin DME ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/special-interests/coding-tuning/145696-ews-sync-issue-with-csl-clone-dme-solved)). Two Touring attempts failed:

- A 2002 325iT with its donor's DME, not virginized: INPA refused the job, Tool32 reported success but the sync status stayed invalid, and fault 130 (0x82) remained ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/ews-synch-for-s54-swap-into-e46-touring.1318881/)).
- A 325xi wagon built in 03/2001, with a Kassel-virginized 2003.5 MSS54: "start value not understood" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/cant-sync-ews-dme-on-s54-swapped-e46-wagon.1300465/)). A member blamed the older car's ZCS coding. That's unlikely: BMW keeps the rolling code separate from ZCS coding, and the MSS54 isn't coded from the vehicle order at all ([BMW ST051](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST051%20Body%20Electronics%20I%20%28Archive%201%29/05%20EWS.pdf), [SGBD documentation](https://github.com/emdzej/ediabasx-docs-sgbd/blob/main/docs/sgbd/MSS54DS0.md)). A partly cleared DME, the wrong INPA script or low voltage fit better.

**Route 3: the donor's EWS and keys.** In the 2002 325iT above, fitting the donor's EWS and key and re-running the Tool32 sync cleared the EWS fault, but the car still cranked without starting, with a fuel-pump relay fault and all six injectors disabled, and the thread ends there ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/ews-synch-for-s54-swap-into-e46-touring.1318881/)). The route also leaves the car needing the donor key's transponder at the ring antenna as well as your own key in the lock, unless a locksmith re-keys the cylinders; your tailgate and rear-glass locks have no M3 equivalent. The donor EWS and its key transponders carry the donor's VIN, which forum members say takes a dedicated key programmer to change ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/chassis-vin-dme-ews-vin-lcm-ike-vin-all-mismatched-help.1253491/)). An SMG donor's EWS may expect a neutral signal rather than your clutch switch, so match its coding to your saved EWS file. Use this route as a test or a fallback.

**If your state checks DME software.** Since July 2021 California's Smog Check fails software that doesn't match an OEM or CARB-approved configuration ([BAR](https://www.bar.ca.gov/pdf/bag/202107/bar-ois-modified-software.pdf)), and a swap shop reports California failing cars for detected tunes ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swap-california-bar-certification.1307461/page-2)). If your state checks software, ask whether a VIN-matched EWS delete passes before you choose a route. See [Registration and legal](#registration-and-legal-us).

### Instrument cluster and mileage

The M3 cluster brings a tachometer with an 8,000 rpm redline and warm-up lights, and an oil-temperature gauge; with the MSS54 they all work ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)).

![E46 M3 instrument cluster with an mph speedometer](photos/m3-cluster.jpg)

*Photo: The M3 cluster, US mph version. Adam Woodford, [CC BY 2.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by/2.0/), via [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:BMW_M3_-_IMG_5105_-_Flickr_-_Adam_Woodford.jpg).*

**Why it needs work.** The cluster and the light module (LCM) both store the VIN and mileage and compare them at every key-on. A mismatch lights the "tamper dot" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)), and until the VINs match the DSC and parking-brake lamps can stay on too ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/e46-m3-cluster-coding.1278842/)). A used M3 cluster carries the donor's VIN and mileage, and neither can be rewritten over the OBD port. PA-Soft can read the cluster, but its VIN and mileage areas are write-protected, and NCS Expert can't write the cluster's VIN at all ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/special-interests/coding-tuning/154645-cluster-with-coding-index-0x24-virginization), [e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/e46-m3-cluster-coding.1278842/)).

**The light module is the master record.** It holds your car's VIN and true mileage and, on cars built from 09/2001, the backup copy of the vehicle order ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-how-to-code-fa-for-at-mt-swap.1290835/)). Make the cluster match the LCM, never the reverse. One swap video advises "realigning" the LCM to a donor cluster ([ShopLifeTV – What parts are needed for an S54 swap (2016), 03:14](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=hnaZqKbvwT4&t=194s)); with a donor cluster, that rewrites your car's mileage record.

Two ways to set the cluster to your VIN and true mileage:

1. **A cluster service.** Send the cluster with your VIN and the mileage your LCM shows. Kassel set Tenza's M3 cluster "to reflect the mileage of the actual wagon" and added the rear-door warning lights that M3 clusters lack. Tenza budgeted about \$1,000 in 2021 for Kassel's DME and cluster work, including shipping ([Tenza Motorsports – M3 Touring build finally runs (2021), 02:17](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8uwpzS_-uDs&t=137s), [Tenza Motorsports – 5 things they don't tell you, part 2 (2021), 06:07](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mDBiRn30fEs&t=367s)). Owners also name Huggins Racing, Fixels and RPM Motorsport ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/), [NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/228697-cluster-replacement)).
2. **A new memory chip, fitted yourself.** Save the cluster's EEPROM with PA-Soft. Prepare a copy with the odometer cleared and your VIN set, and write it to a new M35080 chip, either in a programmer such as the R270 or by soldering in a blank chip and writing it with PA-Soft. With the VIN matching, the refitted cluster reads your true mileage from the LCM within seconds. One S54 wagon owner did exactly this ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)). It needs hot-air desoldering, and the gauge needles come off and must be reset. The steps are in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#session-4-cluster-and-light-module).

**Cars built before 09/2001** store their options in the older ZCS format, and the light module may not pass the mileage to a new cluster. One 2001 Touring owner had the true reading written straight into the chip ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)); a cluster service can do the same.

**Coding.** Keep the M3 cluster's own coding index. The redesigned clusters from about 2003 (index 23 or 24) are the only ones that properly support the M Track light and the brake-force display ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)). Change single options in the cluster's coding file rather than default-coding it: one owner default-coded a cluster whose index had been altered, and its tach needle stuck at 9,000 rpm until he wrote back his EEPROM backup (same source). The options that matter:

- **The three lamp switches:** with the lamp wires in place, code `CAN_OELDRUCKSCHALTER`, `GENERATOR_UEBER_CAN` and `TOG_UEBER_CAN` to `nicht_aktiv`, on an MS43 or an MS45.1 Touring. The oil-pressure, charge and oil-level lamps then come from the wires, whatever software the DME runs. An MSS54HP didn't light the oil-pressure lamp over CAN even with the cluster coded for it ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/61405-why-does-my-oil-light-turn-off-with-ignition-ii-engine-off), [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#cluster-wiring)).
- **Fuel-gauge curve:** `TANK_KENNLINIE` at `60_l_literskala`, for a plastic tank; our reading of the coding list ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#fuel-gauge-and-tank-senders)).
- **Oil-level readout:** leave it off. The trip computer's oil-level display (`OELNIVEAU_M3`) works only on an MSS54HP running the Euro-only CSL software, and this build keeps stock US software. The low-oil warning lamp is set by the DME and works without it ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/special-interests/coding-tuning/17863-how-do-you-code-csl-oil-level-indicator)).
- **M Track light:** optional; the mode works without it ([ECUWorx](https://www.ecuworx.co.uk/uploads/M-Track.pdf)).

**Touring details.**

- M3 clusters have no rear-door lights. Solder them in, or have the service add them ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-m3-cluster-into-non-m-retrofit.1277945/)).
- The backlight feed changed at 09/2002: a cluster from an earlier car fitted to a car built from 09/2002 needs a backlight wire to cluster pin 7 (same source). A cluster from a 2003-on donor avoids this.
- The speedometer over-reads by about 8.9% by design. The M3 cluster's correction table is set to 49/45, against about 5.7% on non-M clusters ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/diy-e46-cluster-gauges-definitions.1324535/)), so it isn't caused by the differential. Check it against GPS; an edit to the cluster's memory can correct it.
- Nobody has reported an M3 cluster on a Touring's tank senders. Check the gauge, the range and Tool32's `STATUS_TANKINHALT_LESEN` against a measured fill before you rely on them. It matters beyond the gauge: the DME takes the fuel level from the cluster for its leak test ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#fuel-gauge-and-tank-senders)).
- Keeping your Touring's cluster also works with the S54; one 323i swapper's stock cluster did ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swap-wiring-information.1003871/)). You lose the M3's tach and warm-up lights.

> [!CAUTION]
> Federal law lets you replace an odometer if the replacement shows the same mileage ([49 U.S.C. §32704](https://www.law.cornell.edu/uscode/text/49/32704)), so the M3 cluster keeps your Touring's true mileage and nothing else. Program it to that mileage, photograph the cluster and LCM readings first, and keep screenshots before and after. Never write a donor cluster's reading into the light module. The federal written mileage disclosure on sale no longer applies to model-year 2010 and older vehicles ([49 CFR 580.17](https://www.law.cornell.edu/cfr/text/49/580.17)), but altering mileage is still illegal and your state may require disclosure.

### DSC, brakes and steering angle

**Which units you have.**

- **Your Touring:** a Teves MK60 on most US 325is, though BMW's catalog also lists ASC-only units for the US 325i Touring, with no steering-angle sensor, so check the hydraulic unit's label ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Touring/USA/325i-M54/L-N/browse/brakes/asc_hydro_unit_control_unit_support/), [e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/how-to-calibrate-steering-angle-using-tool32.1282261/)). From 09/2001 the Touring's MK60 (34516765453) uses a 47-pin plug and a yaw-sensor cluster that talks over CAN; earlier MK60s use older analogue sensors ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Touring/USA/325i-M54/L-N/browse/brakes/hydro_unit_dsc_fastening_sensors_2/), [e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/mk60-coding-index-the-following-control-unit-is-not-allowed-for-this-coding-job.1264623/)).
- **The M3:** a Teves MK20 (34512229827, 43-pin plug, separate pre-charge pump) on cars built before 09/2002, then an MK60 (34512282558, or 34512282421 on Competition cars from 12/2004) with the same 47-pin plug and yaw sensor as your car ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Coupe/USA/M3-S54/L-N/browse/brakes/hydro_unit_dsc_control_unit_fastening/), [ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Coupe/USA/M3-S54/L-N/browse/brakes/hydro_unit_dsc_fastening_sensors/)). The MK20 won't plug into your harness.

**Option A (recommended): keep your Touring's MK60, booster and master cylinder.** It needs no coding tricks, and it's the documented fix. One S54 wagon never cleared "VIN not initialized" with an M3 MK60, and refitting its own 325i unit cured it ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-cluster-swap-dsc-light.1260275/)). Non-M MK60s work with the MSS54 once the cluster, steering-angle sensor and DSC share the car's VIN ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/kalims-s54-sedan-swap-warning-pic-heavy.889867/page-38)). The cost: the unit stays coded for a 325i (M54 engine, 325i brakes and rack, open differential), no source says how that behaves with the M3's brakes, rack and limited-slip differential, and you give up M Track.

- Fit the M3's front brake hoses (34322229723, an M3-only part) with the M3 calipers. Your brake pipes stay: from 09/2002 the M3 uses the same front pipes as your Touring, and the rear pipes and hoses are the same parts on both cars. The M3 pipes 34322282275/277 belong to M3s built before 09/2002, with the MK20 ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/34322229723/), [Brakes and bleeding](#brakes-and-bleeding)).
- Nobody has reported the pedal feel of M3 calipers on the Touring's master cylinder. Bleed, test and judge.

**Option B: the M3's MK60 (donors built from 09/2002).** Another S54 wagon ran on both units and was "much better with m-track mode and M3 settings" ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/special-interests/coding-tuning/172815-flashing-the-mk60-with-zcp-software/page2)). It brings:

- **The M3 master cylinder** (34317838638 from 09/2002) and perhaps **the M3 booster** (34332282622 from 09/2002), in place of your own ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/34332282622/)); both cars' numbers are in [Brakes and bleeding](#brakes-and-bleeding). Nobody has documented whether the M3 master cylinder suits your booster. In a 330Ci swap the M3 booster's bolt holes didn't line up with the firewall and had to be slotted ([DBO – Full M3 swap into a 330Ci, part 1 (2019), 08:44](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=td8MgQ_3KGs&t=524s)), and its vacuum hose connects on the other side ([Tenza Motorsports – 5 things they don't tell you, part 1 (2021), 07:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-TXzg2Z8d7w&t=460s)).
- **Coding by hand.** NCS Expert codes every MK60 from one rule file that picks M3 values only when the vehicle order says `S54B32`. Your Touring's says `M54B25` and `TOUR`, so an M3 unit gets 325i values unless you set M3 values yourself ([liam821](https://liam821.com/MK60-Comparison.pdf)). The M3 unit's coding has no Touring body type, only coupe and convertible ([ECUWorx](https://www.ecuworx.co.uk/uploads/M-Track.pdf)). Nobody has documented an M3 MK60 coded into a Touring step by step.
- **On a Touring built before 09/2001,** the later yaw-sensor cluster and its wiring as well ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/mk60-coding-index-the-following-control-unit-is-not-allowed-for-this-coding-job.1264623/)).
- **M Track,** switched on by coding and worked by the existing DSC button. A cluster with the M Track symbol is optional ([ECUWorx](https://www.ecuworx.co.uk/uploads/M-Track.pdf)).

> [!TIP]
> Whichever you choose, keep your Touring's MK60, booster, master cylinder and cluster until the car has passed inspection, with the rest of the hold list in [Space, storage and logistics](#space-storage-and-logistics). They're your fallback.

**Wheel-speed sensors.** An M3 built before 09/2002 has the MK20, with inductive sensors and gray plugs: they don't plug into your harness, and an MK60 isn't made to read them. From 09/2002 the M3 has the same active sensors with blue plugs as your Touring ([BMW ST056](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST056%20Chassis%20Dynamics%20%28Archive%201%29/04%2520E46%2520DSC%25201-27-03.pdf)). BMW says the MK60 uses the same pulse wheels as the MK20, and the current front (34526792896) and rear (34526752683) sensors are listed for both cars, so your own sensors should read the M3 hubs ([ETK: front](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/34526792896/), [ETK: rear](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/34526752683/)). That fits the builds: Tenza's 2004 Touring had a 2002 donor, so its sensors' plugs didn't mate, and non-M sensors in the M3 hubs gave an ABS light at first start that was never diagnosed ([Tenza Motorsports – 5 things they don't tell you, part 2 (2021), 03:15](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mDBiRn30fEs&t=195s), [Tenza Motorsports – M3 Touring build finally runs (2021), 07:18](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8uwpzS_-uDs&t=438s)), while a 2003 xi Touring took an M3's sensors without trouble ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-touring-project.1240087/)). Never splice an MK20 donor's sensor leads onto your harness. Check the plugs on both cars before you order, watch all four wheel speeds in INPA's DSC status while rolling, and read the DSC fault memory after the first slow drive ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#wheel-speed-and-pad-wear-sensors)). NAM3 lists the MK60's wheel-speed faults in English ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/special-interests/coding-tuning/167185-mk-60-fault-codes-in-english)).

**Coding and VIN.** Once the cluster carries your VIN, code the DSC (Option B only) and write your VIN into it with PA-Soft. Then initialise the VIN and steering angle in INPA, and run the three compensation routines with the wheel straight, no brake pressure and the car level ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/how-to-change-abs-asc-control-module-trifecta-5df5-error-e46.1275927/), written on a non-US 2002 318i whose INPA pages are the same; [NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/189096-mk20-mk60-swap)). The steps and the fallbacks (Tool32, a scan tool) are in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#session-5-dsc-coding-and-vin). **Watch:** [LM Auto Repairs – E46 MK60 coding and VIN (2016), 04:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=1dn4c3g6dsA&t=287s) for the PA-Soft VIN write (filmed on a 2003 E46 diesel), and [50sKid – E46 DSC unit replacement and coding (2016), 33:47](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=CvyVCwCAx1Q&t=2027s) for the initialisation.

**Steering angle after the alignment.** Calibrate the steering-angle sensor again after the wheel alignment, with the wheel dead straight. The calibration takes the wheel's position as zero, and a calibrated sensor should read 0 with the wheel straight ([Evan Goyuk – Calibrate the steering-angle sensor (2020), 03:19](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=lseY6SRXWks&t=199s)), so a sensor zeroed before the toe was set reads off-center afterwards. Use INPA's MK60 page, or Tool32 if your INPA lacks it, as some 5.0.x builds do ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/dsc-mk60-replacement-coding-with-ncs-expert.1286178/)). See [Wheel alignment](#wheel-alignment).

**Bleeding and testing.** A replaced unit, or one that has run dry, needs INPA's pump routine as well as a conventional bleed; see [Brakes and bleeding](#brakes-and-bleeding). Drive only once the pedal is firm, test the ABS with a careful hard stop on a quiet road, and bleed again after the first few drives ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/189096-mk20-mk60-swap)).

![DSC and brake options](diagrams/13-dsc-brake-options.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 12. The DSC and brake choice. Only a donor built from 09/2002 has an MK60 that plugs into your harness, so with an earlier donor you keep your own unit (route A, the default). Either way your brake pipes stay, the M3 calipers take new M3 front hoses, and the wheel-speed sensor plugs need checking.*

If your car turns out to be a 325xi, it has a Bosch DSC 5.7 instead, and Option A doesn't exist ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/how-to-calibrate-steering-angle-using-tool32.1282261/)).

### Inspection readiness

- **Keep every emissions function.** Order the DME flash as an EWS delete only. Keep the secondary-air pump, all four O2 sensors and the DM-TL leak-test pump, wired to the MSS54's pins 18, 20 and 30 ([Kassel](https://www.kasselperformance.com/product/bmw-e46-m3-s54-mss54-hp-tuning/), [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#dm-tl-leak-test-pump-test-before-you-swap)).
- **Write your full VIN into the DME.** State testers read the VIN stored in the DME, and BMW issued a bulletin for E46s whose DME held only the last seven digits ([BMW SI B12 09 13](https://static.nhtsa.gov/odi/tsbs/2013/MC-10150160-9999.pdf)). California's test reads it from the OBD port ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/chassis-vin-dme-ews-vin-lcm-ike-vin-all-mismatched-help.1253491/)).
- **What the federal OBD check passes.** A car with stored codes passes if the check-engine lamp isn't commanded on, and a model-year 2001-or-later car may pass with one monitor not ready. It fails if the lamp is commanded on but not lit, or doesn't light with the key on and the engine off ([40 CFR 85.2222](https://www.ecfr.gov/current/title-40/chapter-I/subchapter-C/part-85/subpart-W/section-85.2222)), so check the bulb with the M3 cluster fitted. States add their own limits: New York fails a 2001-or-later car with more than one monitor not ready ([NYVIP3](https://www.nyvip3.com/OBDII/ReadyVSNotReady)). Massachusetts doesn't emissions-test a 2001–05 car in 2026 ([Massachusetts](#massachusetts)).
- **Expect stored faults:** location 1 (0x01), the pump fault, which doesn't light the check-engine lamp, and possibly 130 (0x82) after the EWS delete. Nobody has documented whether a leftover 130 (0x82) commands the lamp, so check `STAT_MIL_EIN` and a generic scanner rather than guess, and ask for the tampering-fault delete with the EWS delete.
- **The EVAP leak test is the hard monitor.** The MSS54 runs its DM-TL test only with the engine off, after at least 20 minutes of driving that followed at least 5 hours parked, with the tank 15–85% full, the air between −7 and 35 °C (20–95 °F) and the battery between 11.5 and 14.5 V, and only every second time those conditions are met ([BMW ST041](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST041%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle/3%20EngineElectronics%20%28Part%203%29.pdf)). EVAP is the monitor reported "not ready" on an MSS54 swap ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/s54-swaps-and-smog-in-california-update.1303447/)).
- **The cluster feeds the leak test.** The DME takes the fuel level, the outside temperature and the parked time from the cluster over CAN ([SGBD documentation](https://github.com/emdzej/ediabasx-docs-sgbd/blob/main/docs/sgbd/MSS54DS0.md)). An M3 cluster that misreads your Touring's tank senders can keep the test from ever running, so check the fuel gauge against a measured fill first ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#the-dm-tl-leak-test)).
- **One bank, and purge under EVAP.** The S54 has one bank of six cylinders, so a scanner shows one set of catalyst and O2 results, not two. A generic scanner doesn't list the purge check on its own; it reports it as part of the EVAP monitor ([manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#what-the-mss54-monitors)).
- **Readiness resets** when codes are cleared or the battery is disconnected ([NYVIP3](https://www.nyvip3.com/OBDII/ReadyVSNotReady)). Lazy pre-cat O2 sensors can stop the secondary-air monitor completing ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/e46-emission-readiness-monitor-issues.1244521/), unverified); BMW does judge the secondary-air system by those sensors' response ([BMW ST041](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST041%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle/3%20EngineElectronics%20%28Part%203%29.pdf)). The drive plan is in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#a-drive-plan-for-readiness).
- **Software checks:** see [Immobilizer (EWS)](#immobilizer-ews) and [Registration and legal](#registration-and-legal-us).

### Troubleshooting

The full trees and tables, with tool jobs, pins and expected readings, are in the [manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#troubleshooting). INPA prints fault numbers in decimal and BMW's job documentation in hex, so this guide gives both: 130 (0x82) is the immobilizer fault, and 1 (0x01) the pump fault.

| Symptom | Likely cause | Where to look |
|---|---|---|
| Won't crank | A flat battery, which stopped Tenza's first attempt ([Tenza Motorsports – M3 Touring build finally runs (2021), 03:55](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=8uwpzS_-uDs&t=235s)); the key not recognized; no clutch signal at EWS pin 8 | [Won't start](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#wont-start), [Immobilizer (EWS)](#immobilizer-ews) |
| Cranks, won't start; 130 (0x82) stored | The DME isn't released: the delete wasn't done, or a virgin DME's sync failed | [Immobilizer (EWS)](#immobilizer-ews) |
| Cranks, won't start; no prime at key-on | X60004 pin 10 not reaching K96, fuse F54 or F3, or the E-box plugs | [Fuel-pump control](#fuel-pump-control) |
| Cranks, won't start; primes, but pressure low | Feed and return swapped at the filter-regulator ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/82513-s54-swap-to-ms42-car-no-start)) | [Fuel system](#fuel-system) |
| Starts, then dies | Not the immobilizer. A throttle-system (EML) fault that shuts the engine off within about 3 seconds is one documented cause ([MotoIQ](https://motoiq.com/project-e46-m3-part-8-the-dreaded-eml-light/)) | [Won't start](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#wont-start) |
| Fault 1 (0x01) stored, but the pump primes and the engine runs | Expected with US software on the relay | [Fuel-pump control](#fuel-pump-control) |
| Injector "circuit low" on all six | Mixed X6053/X60531 halves | [Harness and re-pinning](#harness-and-re-pinning) |
| No oil or charge lamp at key-on | Lamp wires not moved or added, or the cluster coded for CAN | [Warning lights](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#warning-lights) |
| Yellow brake lamp, or fault 106 (0x6A) and no cruise | The brake switch's test-contact wire still on X60004 pin 28 | [Harness and re-pinning](#harness-and-re-pinning) |
| Cruise won't engage | The non-M clutch module, the gear switch, the X6011 pin-7 crossing or the brake contacts | [Harness and re-pinning](#harness-and-re-pinning) |
| Revs hang between shifts, or idle hunts on clutch-in | Clutch-chain wiring first ("the most common rev hang"), then SMG software in the DME. In a non-M automatic-to-manual swap (2000 323Ci, MS42) it was a tune written for the automatic ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/manual-swap-rev-hang.1315205/)) | [Manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#driving-emissions-and-comfort) |
| Limp mode or an SMG lamp | An SMG donor's DME still running SMG software | [Immobilizer (EWS)](#immobilizer-ews) |
| Tamper dot | Cluster VIN differs from the LCM's, or the LCM's mileage is higher ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/228697-cluster-replacement)) | [Instrument cluster and mileage](#instrument-cluster-and-mileage) |
| DSC lamp with "VIN not initialized" | VIN and steering angle not initialised, or an M3 MK60 that won't accept the Touring | [DSC, brakes and steering angle](#dsc-brakes-and-steering-angle) |
| ABS lamp after the first drive | An MK20 donor's wheel-speed sensors, or a sensor not seated | [DSC, brakes and steering angle](#dsc-brakes-and-steering-angle) |
| Sport LED dark; fault 110 (0x6E) | The wire from the switch center to X60004 pin 16 | [Manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#sport-button-and-switch-center) |
| EVAP monitor never ready | The heater not on pin 18, the pump and valve wiring not proven, a hose or the filler cap, the fuel gauge, or the test conditions | [Inspection readiness](#inspection-readiness) |
| A/C won't engage | The cluster not passing the request, then the chain from the DME's pin 29 to relay K19 and the compressor clutch | [Manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md#driving-emissions-and-comfort) |
| Speedometer about 9% high | The M3 cluster's built-in over-read | [Instrument cluster and mileage](#instrument-cluster-and-mileage) |

## Deep dive: bodywork and fitment

Everything in the M3 front end bolts to the Touring except the front fenders. The rear arches are the big job: you graft M3 arch steel into the Touring's rear quarters around a mock-up of the final axle, wheels and tires. Set up the welder, and protect yourself and the car, as described in [Welding settings and safety](#welding-settings-and-safety).

### Front end on a 4-door body

Genuine M3 front fenders don't bolt onto a sedan or Touring: the coupe's front fenders are "an inch longer than those on the sedan and wagon" ([Jalopnik](https://www.jalopnik.com/an-e46-m3-wagon-would-have-been-the-best-bmw-1807152574/)). Builders describe the M3 fender as longer between the arch and the door and sitting further out; forced into place, its swage line drops, and its side gill sits further back than a 4-door fender's ([Tracks and Trails – What it really takes (2026), 02:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=140s), [Calvin's Car Diary – PT9 (2020), 06:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=360s)).

The rest bolts on. "Basically the complete M3 frontend will fit on the Touring… the only thing that won't fit are the two front fenders" ([M3projekts – Ep3 (2023), 08:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TT0sd2JB5dY&t=480s)), and Redish found the M3 hood "fits straight on" ([Autoalex Cars – Part 3 (2023), 05:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cwbRDG7XHa8&t=300s)). The hood still needs M3-shaped fenders beside it, which is why one budget Touring kept a non-M front end ([Tenza Motorsports – Wagon tour and plans (2020), 01:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=roTBxPmyhEY&t=100s)). BMW's catalog lists the same front panel (51717111691) and front body brackets for the Touring, coupe and M3 ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/51717111691/)), and the same M3 fender (41357894337) and hood (41617894223) for the M3 coupe, convertible and CSL, so a convertible donor's front panels are the same parts ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41357894337/)).

![E46 M3 front fender with its side gill, above a double-spoke wheel](photos/m3-fender-gill.jpg)

*Photo: The M3 front fender, with its flared arch and side gill (here on a convertible). JanST, [CC BY-SA 2.5](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/2.5/), via [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:BMW_M3_E46_Cabrio_detail.JPG).*

![Front fender graft](diagrams/07-front-fender-graft.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 13. The fender graft. Keep the rear strip of your 4-door fender, which carries the door gap, A-pillar flange and top mounting rail, and join it to the front of the M3 fender with its flared arch and gill.*

**Option A: graft genuine M3 fenders.** Keep a strip of your 4-door fender at the door gap and A-pillar, and weld the front of the M3 fender to it ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/e46-2001-2006/75083-e46-touring-m3-front-end-conversion)); one builder describes "a slit off the sedan fender cut and welded on to the M3 fender" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/can-m3-hood-fenders-front-bumper-be-fitted-to-an-e46-sedan.191582/)). SOS Customz kept the factory fender's mounting points, door-gap section and front corner, tacked the M3 flare to it and spliced the M3 gill molding in so its line meets the headlight ([SOS Customz – Building an E46 M3 Touring wagon (2023), 05:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L8m9gEOklVY&t=300s), [12:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L8m9gEOklVY&t=720s)). Calvin's body man used the rear section of a standard Touring fender and moved the gill forward ([Calvin's Car Diary – PT9 (2020), 07:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=420s)). A graft that joins the front half of an M3 fender to the rear half of the original, so the arch line stops short, looks wrong ([Calvin's Car Diary – PT5 (2020), 07:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=KzgXjYYyaL0&t=440s)). On a bench:

1. Bolt your 4-door fender to the car and mark a graft line a few inches ahead of the door gap, clear of the arch flare.
2. Trial-fit the M3 fender with the hood, headlight and bumper mounted, so the front edges sit where they must. Mark the same line on it.
3. Cut both fenders, leaving the M3 piece slightly long, then trim to a 1 mm butt gap.
4. Clamp both pieces back on the car, check the door gap and the line along the hood, and tack-weld. If pushing the fender flush lifts its top edge, relieve it; one builder slit the top of the fender ([Eurofed Automotive – Miki's M3 Touring (2025), 04:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=iolOysUs20M&t=240s)).
5. Remove the fender, finish with short stitch welds, alternating ends to keep heat down, and planish the seam.
6. Seal the back of the seam and prime.

**Option B: buy Touring fenders.**

- **2M Autowerks (steel).** One owner's verdict: "The fit is ok. Not what I would call factory appearance." Thin mounting tabs broke, the line at the A-pillar "doesn't look right", it needed filler for "dimples and fixing some lines", and the M3 liners needed "a LOT of trimming" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/2m-m3-touring-conversion-fenders.1209713/)).
- **M3Projekts (carbon).** €1,400 before VAT; the maker says you can "simply bolt on" them ([M3Projekts](https://m3projekts.com/product/e46-m3-touring-front-fenders/)). They're moulded from a metal hybrid of sedan and M3 coupe fenders ([M346 Projects – Pt 18 (2024), 02:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IBAqCYQuEzs&t=140s)). Fit reports: "a few hours of fiddling" per side, with lines and door gaps close but one side tight at the front and open at the rear ([Tracks and Trails – What it really takes (2026), 04:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=240s)); two days of alignment, a carbon tab ground shorter and the hood-hinge holes opened 2–3 mm ([M3projekts – Ep4 (2023), 04:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=_sZHTK-Xae4&t=270s)); about five hours to fit the side-gill trim and modify its clips ([M346 Projects – Pt 18 (2024), 07:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=IBAqCYQuEzs&t=420s)).

**Before you commit:**

- **Bumper supports.** The M3 bumper mounts on fender supports 51118195295/96. Tourings built before 09/2001 have them; facelift cars use different supports and need these added ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/51118195295/), [Garagistic](https://www.garagistic.com/products/e46-m3-style-front-bumper-aftermarket-replacement)). Early M3s (to 11/2001) share the Touring's bumper carrier; later ones use a lighter carrier (51117893527). Facelift Tourings with genuine M3 bumpers and headlights exist; the Sloan car is a 2004 ([BMWBlog](https://www.bmwblog.com/2024/11/11/custom-e46-m3-touring-wagon-s54-build/)).
- **Brackets and liners.** A Touring without the M Tech front bumper needs two more brackets for the bumper corners, and you need the M3's front and rear fender liners ([M3projekts – Ep3 (2023), 09:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TT0sd2JB5dY&t=570s)). The 2M fender owner also needed the M3 bumper carrier, lower headlight trim and M3 horn bracket ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/2m-m3-touring-conversion-fenders.1209713/)).
- **Airflow.** Keep the M3 bumper's lower opening and ducting; the oil cooler depends on it. With a ZHP bumper, Tenza had to cut away a scoop behind the bumper so the M3 oil cooler got air ([Tenza Motorsports – Front end with M3 parts (2021), 03:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=k2YMcy9TdJk&t=200s)).
- **Washer tank.** The M3's washer tank sits low behind the bumper. Original M3 and ZHP bumpers leave room for it; aftermarket bumpers may not ([Tenza Motorsports – 5 things (part 2) (2021), 01:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=mDBiRn30fEs&t=60s)). Its pump and level-sensor leads need extending ([M3projekts – Ep5 (2023), 04:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Ipbjt8S2c2g&t=270s)); see the [Wiring and coding manual](WIRING-AND-CODING.md).

### Rear arches and doors

**How much wider.** The rear hub faces sit in about the same place on both cars. The M3's rear track is 1,525 mm on Motorsport Australia's homologation form and 60.0 in (1,524 mm) on BMW USA's spec sheet, and the US 325i Touring's is 1,483 mm ([Motorsport Australia](https://motorsport.org.au/wpblob0fe832abcb/wp-content/uploads/docs/default-source/sporting-technical/group-3e/3-18-007-bmw-e46-m3.pdf), [BMW USA: M3](https://www.press.bmwgroup.com/usa/article/attachment/T0022127EN_US/41385), [BMW USA: Touring](https://www.press.bmwgroup.com/usa/article/attachment/T0022146EN_US/41437)). Half the track plus the wheel offset puts each rear hub face about 788–790 mm from the centerline on both cars: 788.5 mm with the M3's ET26 wheel and an ET47 wheel on the Touring. So the extra rear width comes from the M3 wheel itself. Its ET26 offset moves the wheel about 21 mm further out per side, and the 9-inch rim and 255 tire add width on top. Treat the hub-face figure as good to a millimetre or two: neither source says which wheels its track was measured on (the form lists the M3's 19-inch ET27 rear wheels, which would give 789.5 mm), and BMW USA gives no offset for the Touring's wheels, so their ET47 is an assumption (unverified). The bodywork follows: HARD Motorsport's M3-style rear flares are "Aprox 30mm wider" ([HARD Motorsport](https://www.hardmotorsport.com/hard-motorsport-bmw-e46-sedan-touring-m3-style-motorsport-fender-flares-rear-set/)), and an owner who fitted them measured "a solid 25-30mm" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/new-hard-motorsport-sedan-m-style-flares-installed.1049334/)). The forward "nose" of the M3 flare also sits wider than a 4-door body at the door shut line, so it has to be beaten in and blended where it meets the door ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 M3 body work (2024), 28:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=1700s)).

![Rear three-quarter view of an E46 Touring showing the standard rear wheel arch](photos/touring-rear.jpg)

*Photo: The standard Touring rear arch, on a facelift 325xi (BMW lists the same rear side panel, 41218242347, for the 325i Touring: [ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41218242347/)). Bull-Doser, public domain, via [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:%2702-%2705_BMW_325xi_Wagon.JPG).*

![Rear view of an E46 M3 coupe showing its flared rear arches and quad exhaust](photos/m3-rear-arch.jpg)

*Photo: The M3's flared rear arch, which this conversion grafts onto the Touring. Cjp24, [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), via [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:BMW_M3_%28E46%29%2C_blue.jpg).*

**Where the steel comes from.** Most conversions we found used welded steel. BMW's prototype arches "appear to be fully metal constructed" ([Redish](https://www.redish-motorsport.com/BMWE46M3TouringS54WideArchBuilds.html)), and the Sloan car's "OEM metal fender flares were grafted on" ([BMWBlog](https://www.bmwblog.com/2024/11/11/custom-e46-m3-touring-wagon-s54-build/)). Petroyle's cars were reported with steel rear quarters ([Rush](https://www.rushmagazine.co.uk/post/petroyle-e46-m3-touring-revealed)), but in 2026 Petroyle said it is moving to carbon panels for production ([Hagerty – Petroyle's E46 M3 Touring (2026), 09:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=--YbwCS4F_g&t=540s)), and one review says its car's arches are carbon ([candyshowroom – The M3 BMW never built (2026), 15:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=uMAVcXRFZU4&t=940s)). BMW's changes for the M3 coupe included extra C-pillar reinforcement, modified inner side sections around the wheel arches, and a fuel-filler flap reshaped to the M3 quarter ([BMW ST041](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST041%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle/1%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle.pdf)), so expect to rebuild the inside of the arch, not just the skin. In Massachusetts, welding steel from another car into your unibody may count as reassembling "the frames or unit bodies of two or more motor vehicles", which brings an RMV inspection and a "reconstructed" title; ask the RMV first, or use new BMW panels ([Massachusetts](#massachusetts)).

What BMW's catalog shows:

- **Two M3 quarters.** The coupe and CSL use 41217890689/690 (14.5 kg); the M3 convertible has its own, 41357894541/542 (11.5 kg) ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41357894541/)). The difference comes from the upper body, C-pillar and roof against the soft-top, as on non-M cars: a 325Ci coupe quarter weighs 15.2 kg and a 325Ci convertible's 10.3 kg ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41357029221/)).
- **Almost certainly the same arch.** BMW gives both M3s the same rear track, the same 18 × 9 rear wheels and the same 70.1 in body width ([BMW USA](https://www.press.bmwgroup.com/usa/article/attachment/T0022127EN_US/41385)). The parts around the flare are also the same on both: filler flap 51172695524, the rear bumper and its side brackets, the side skirts and their intermediate pieces, and the M3-only "coverings" 51712695667/51717895936 ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/51172695524/)). Nobody has published a measured comparison.
- **The inner half of the rear wheelhouse (41148231757/758) is one part on the M3 coupe, the M3 convertible and the US 325i Touring** ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41148231757/)). The extra width sits in the outer skin and the outer side of the wheelhouse. The Touring has its own outer half (41218242355/356); the matching structure is the interior side frame (41217890677/678) on the M3 coupe, and the interior side frame (41217894557/558) and inner lateral trim panel (41217894553/554) on the convertible. That is our reading of BMW's diagrams ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Cabrio/USA/M3-S54/L-N/browse/bodywork/rear_wheelhouse_floor_parts/)), not a BMW statement.

**A convertible donor** yields the whole M3 outer skin from the door shut line back to the tail-light, with the full flare and the right-side filler opening and flap. It lacks only the C-pillar and roof section, which a Touring doesn't use. It also brings the same M3 trunk floor (41127895619), front fenders, hood, rear bumper, side skirts and filler flap as a coupe. The catch is cutting: the arch flange sits on the convertible's heavy interior side frame (33.5 kg each, against 5.7 kg on the coupe), so you drill out more spot welds to free it ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41217894557/)). Builds with convertible donors:

- M346 Projects cut both rear quarter sections and the trunk floor from a 2003 M3 convertible and welded them to a Touring, with no convertible-specific problem mentioned on camera ([M346 Projects – Pt 1 (2023), 06:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=YqM2rQw1HQI&t=400s), [Pt 14 (2024), 58:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=3480s)). M3Projekts, which built its own Touring from a convertible donor, says "Coupe or Cabrio/Vert doesn't really matter" ([M3projekts – Ep7 (2023), description](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=ihD3-3ZlBjI)).
- Two written build logs used convertible donors without saying where the arch steel ended up: the US 325iT built in Hungary by M3Projekts, which came back with welded steel arches carried onto the doors ([NAM3](https://nam3forum.com/forums/forum/main-forum/member-build-journals/155763-e46-m3-touring-conversion-to-europe-and-back)), and a 2016 PistonHeads build that bought the rear half of a Cat C 2005 M3 convertible for its quarters and boot floor, then stopped before any bodywork ([PistonHeads](https://www.pistonheads.com/gassing/topic.asp?h=0&f=47&t=1623927&i=60)).

**Steel sources.** Prices were checked in October 2026 unless dated.

| Source | What you get | Price seen | Notes |
|---|---|---|---|
| Your donor's arches, coupe or convertible | Outer skin, inner lip, filler flap and surround | Comes with the donor | First choice. E46 arch rust starts at the inner lip, hidden under seam sealer ([Redish](https://www.redish-motorsport.com/BMWE46M3RearWheelArchesRust.html)); Autoalex's coupe donor lost its inner arch to rust as soon as it was stripped ([Autoalex Cars – Part 1 (2023), 07:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=RyoFFLppseE&t=460s)) |
| Used pre-cut M3 quarter sections | Varies | Varies | Earlier research found them on eBay; no current listing checked |
| New M3 coupe quarter, right 41217890690 (left 41217890689) | Whole side panel, up to the roof rail | \$5,525 for the right ([R44](https://r44performance.com/products/genuine-bmw-e46-m3-rear-quarter-panel-right-41217890690)); no price found for the left; "2,300 and something pounds each side" in the UK in 2023 ([Autoalex Cars – Part 3 (2023), 06:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=cwbRDG7XHa8&t=360s)) | BMW lists them for the coupe and CSL |
| New M3 convertible quarter 41357894541/542 | Side panel to the belt line, with the whole arch and filler opening | About \$2,390–\$2,610 each at US dealers, and one retailer showed about \$1,150 after discount ([BMW of South Atlanta](https://parts.bmwofsouthatlanta.com/oem-parts/bmw-quarter-panel-41357894542), unverified) | Probably the cheapest new outer skin; the inner flange still has to come from a donor, from BMW or from your own fabrication |
| BMW inner wheel-arch panels | Inner arch | £990 each (2025) | No longer available in 2024, back in 2025; Redish doesn't give the part number ([Redish](https://www.redish-motorsport.com/BMWE46M3RearWheelArchesRust.html)) |
| LBL Panels (UK) M3 arch sections | 1.2 mm mild-steel arch, standard or full | £225 / £375 ([m3cutters](https://forums.m3cutters.co.uk/threads/e46-m3-rear-arch-repair-panel.252420/), unverified) | Coupe or convertible fitment not stated |
| Generic E46 coupe/convertible arch repair panels | Non-M arch | From €57 | Listed for "All models except M3" ([Mecatechnic](https://www.mecatechnic.com/en-GB/p/left-rear-wing-arch-for-bmw-e46-coupe-convertible_BT10193)); they don't fit |
| Arch sections cut from E46 front fenders | Front-fender arch welded to the rear quarter | Cheap | One 330d Touring owner's budget route ([Driftworks](https://www.driftworks.com/forum/threads/bmw-e46-330d-touring-wide-rear-arches-m3-19-wheels.249761/), unverified) |

**Check the steel before you cut.**

- **Shape.** Compare the donor's flare with the shape you want: a profile gauge or cardboard templates at three or four stations across both flares, or a test fit of an M3 rear bumper end and the filler flap.
- **Damage.** M346's donor quarters had been in an accident and curled in at the bottom; they needed a fender roller, and the rear bumper had to move in and forward to meet the new flares ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 58:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=3520s)).
- **Margin.** Cut generously: from the door shut line to the bumper line, and from the sill to well above the crown, keeping the factory spot-welded lip intact. If you'll graft the donor's inner tub, "make sure to cut as much of the wheel tub as possible… if you don't have that you're going to have a gap" ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 33:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=1980s)). On the right side, take the filler opening and flap.

![Rear quarter cut lines](diagrams/08-rear-quarter.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 14. Rear quarter, right side. Cut around the arch far enough out to take the donor's whole bulge plus a flat margin, and take the M3 filler flap with it. The bulge's front edge reaches the rear door's shut line: stop there, as BMW did, or reshape the door edge.*

![Arch section and wheel offsets](diagrams/09-arch-section.svg) <!-- portrait -->

*Figure 15. Rear view of a section through the arch, drawn to scale. Both cars' hub faces sit in the same place. The M3's ET26 rear wheel moves the tire 21 mm out and its wider rim adds more, so the outer skin moves out about 25–30 mm and the inner wheelhouse lip is extended to meet it.*

### Measure and mock up before you cut

The arches are welded around this mock-up for good, so have the final springs, dampers, wheels and tires in hand first; the choices are in [Suspension for Touring weight](#suspension-for-touring-weight). It matters: on the M3's springs and dampers, Tenza's Touring, which kept its standard arches, sat so low that its wider rear tires rubbed ([Tenza Motorsports – Coilover install (2022), 00:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=Sit-SmfU-9k&t=40s)). We found no BMW ride-height figure, so take the targets from your donor.

**1. Measure the donor before you strip it** ([Phase 1](#phase-1-buy-and-inspect-the-donor)). With the M3 on its wheels on level ground and its tires at placard pressure, measure at each rear corner (and the front corners too, for reference):

- the height of the hub center above the floor;
- the height of the arch lip straight above the hub center;
- the height of a body point the Touring shares, such as the floor beside a rear subframe mount; the rear axle carrier panel there is the same part on both cars, 41117000246 ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/41117000246/));
- how far the lip sits outboard of the tire's outer face, using a plumb line from the lip.

Subtract to get hub-to-lip and hub-to-body for each side. Note the fuel level and photograph the tire placard.

**2. Record the Touring before any welding.** Note the ride height at all four corners (Phase 0) and the rear door gaps (feeler gauges and photos), then measure the body:

- **Body diagonals.** Pick fixed points you can find again, such as the four rear subframe mounts and the front subframe mounting points, and tape the two diagonals across each set. Measure again after every welding session, in Phase 5 as well as here; a change means the floor or body has pulled. BMW's body dimension data is in TIS, group 51 00 ([newTIS](https://www.newtis.info/)).

**3. Mock up ([Phase 6](#phase-6-metalwork-2-rear-arches)).**

1. Bolt the M3 rear axle in with the dampers you'll run, the brakes, and the final wheels and tires at road pressure. Leave the springs out.
2. Jack each rear hub until hub-to-body matches the donor's figure, or your own target if your springs will set the car higher or lower. With the springs out, the shell's missing weight doesn't matter: you set the suspension position directly instead of trying to load a stripped shell to ride height. Make sure the light shell can't lift off its stands as you jack.
3. Mark the new lip on the quarter: the donor's hub-to-lip height above the hub center, at its offset outboard of the tire's outer face.
4. Check symmetry. Measure from each side's lip mark to the same center point on the floor, such as the middle of the rear axle carrier panel, and compare the two sides.
5. **Full bump.** Jack the hub until the damper's bump stop is fully compressed. Turn the wheel by hand and check that the tire clears the inner wheelhouse, the brake lines and the planned lip. Mark the tire's outer face and top on the body; the new arch has to clear this mark with room for the sidewall's bulge.
6. **Filler neck (right side).** Hold the donor's filler surround at the marks and check that the Touring's filler neck still reaches the M3 filler pot (see step 8 of the graft).
7. Remove the mock-up axle before you weld.

### Arch graft step by step

Weld as described in [Welding settings and safety](#welding-settings-and-safety), with fume extraction, a fire watch, and the fuel tank, filler neck and every fuel and EVAP part near the right-rear arch removed first. ETK lists a fuel expansion-tank bracket (41148196386) among the rear wheelhouse parts, so look inside the quarter for tanks and vent lines before you cut ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Touring/USA/325i-M54/L-N/browse/bodywork/rear_wheelhouse_floor_parts/)).

1. **Strip the area.** Remove the fuel tank and filler neck (the right-side neck runs past the arch), the rear seats, trim, door cards, liners and any wiring in the quarter. Protect the glass from sparks. Grind the sound-deadening mat back about an inch from the weld line so its glue doesn't contaminate the weld ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 41:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=2480s)).
2. **Lay out the cut line** on your quarter a little outside the donor's bulge, so the joint sits on flat panel, where it's easier to weld and finish. SOS Customz ran its cut across the quarter to meet the door at the shut line, lined it up with the filler-door area, and left a lip on the old panel to weld to ([SOS Customz (2023), 02:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L8m9gEOklVY&t=120s)). Transfer the line to the donor section.
3. **Prepare the donor section.** Drill out spot welds instead of cutting where you can, and cut the section slightly oversize. To graft the donor's inner tub (step 5), separate its inner and outer skins by drilling their spot welds ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 27:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=1640s)). Grind the factory seam sealer off the donor's rolled lip, because it burns and spoils the weld ground ([13:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=820s)), and grind off the zinc coating where you'll weld ([11:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=660s)). One builder cut off the donor quarter's tail-light pocket because the Touring's tail-lights are longer ([vTuned garage – M3 wagon quarter swap (2025), 07:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5HE5jgFW-oo&t=440s)).
4. **Cut the outer skin and trial-fit.** Tracks and Trails drilled the spot welds and removed only the outer skin, keeping as much healthy Touring wheelhouse as possible ([Tracks and Trails – What it really takes (2026), 12:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=760s)). Trim until the donor section sits flush with a 1 mm butt gap, as Tracks and Trails did ([15:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=940s)). SOS Customz instead formed a step (joggle) in the old panel and plug-welded the new section to it through punched holes ([SOS Customz (2023), 14:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L8m9gEOklVY&t=840s)). Clamp or cleco the section, and check it against the tire marks and the door.
5. **Join the inner wheelhouse.** This is the hardest joint. On a 325Ci coupe, one forum member called joining the inner fender to the wider M3 arch "the biggest issue" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-rear-quarter-replacements.1305471/)). Two methods have been shown on Tourings:
   - **Extend the Touring's inner lip.** The Touring's inner well "comes down a little bit lower" than the donor flare. Tracks and Trails bonded lip to lip with panel bond, hammered one section out into a new lip, bonded that to the flare and welded the rest, with weld-through primer on the back first ([Tracks and Trails (2026), 17:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=1060s)). A sedan builder sliced the inner lip, bent and hammered it out to meet the outer panel, and welded the quarter back together ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/new-hard-motorsport-sedan-m-style-flares-installed.1049334/)).
   - **Graft the donor's inner tub.** M346 bent the Touring's inner-tub lip, which "was originally pointed straight down", up with a hammer and welded the M3 tub to it in one piece ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 30:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=1820s)).

   Either way, the lip must end up a sealed, double-thickness edge like the factory one. In the factory joint the inner and outer arches meet in a spot-welded channel, and that is where E46 arch rust starts ([Redish](https://www.redish-motorsport.com/BMWE46M3RearWheelArchesRust.html)).
6. **Weld the outer seam in tacks**, about 25–40 mm apart, then fill between them in short stitches, moving around the panel so no area gets hot. Thin panel can't be welded continuously without distorting; stitch it and check penetration from inside ([Calvin's Car Diary – PT9 (2020), 12:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=760s)). Let it cool between passes. Keep welds away from the tail-light pocket if you can, and accept more filler there ([vTuned garage (2025), 09:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=5HE5jgFW-oo&t=540s)).
7. **Close the gap at the sill.** Where the new quarter meets the sill by the door shut, M346 found "a moon-shaped hole" and filled it with a fabricated plate and many small welds ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 46:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=2760s)). Tracks and Trails templated the gaps with tape, cut gussets from scrap and tacked them in ([Tracks and Trails (2026), 23:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=1400s)).
8. **Fuel filler (right side).** Graft the M3's flap and surround with the arch. The M3 flap's bracket holes sit in different places because its door is a different shape; some builders slot the holes, but M346 grafted the M3 surround in, matching its radius ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 37:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=2220s)). The M3 filler pot (51718208646, the same on both M3s) and the flap end up about 25–30 mm further outboard. The flap hinge differs: 51172695527 on the coupe, 51178151955 on the convertible ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/E46-Cabrio/USA/M3-S54/L-N/browse/bodywork/side_panel_tail_trim/)). Before you close the quarter, check that the Touring's filler neck still reaches the pot and seals. The DM-TL leak test finds leaks as small as 1.0 mm ([BMW ST041 Part 3](https://archive.org/download/BMWTechnicalTrainingDocuments/ST041%20M3%20Complete%20Vehicle/3%20EngineElectronics%20%28Part%203%29.pdf)), so a poor seal fails the EVAP monitor (see [Inspection readiness](#inspection-readiness)).
9. **Wheel-arch liners.** The widened arches need liners that fit them, so take the donor's. M3projekts lists rear liners among the parts the conversion needs ([M3projekts – Ep3 (2023), 09:30](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=TT0sd2JB5dY&t=570s)). BMW lists M3-only "coverings", 51712695667 left and 51717895936 right, on both M3s; they may be the rear liners, but we couldn't confirm it ([ETK](https://bmwfans.info/parts-catalog/51712695667/)).
10. **Finish.** Grind welds flush without thinning the panel, seal every seam inside and out, epoxy-prime, and cavity-wax the inside of the quarter. Tracks and Trails scuffed with 100 grit, then used 2K epoxy primer, filler, more epoxy and high-build primer, with seam sealer and Wool Wax inside ([Tracks and Trails (2026), 24:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=1460s), [25:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=1500s)). M346 put weldable zinc primer on all exposed metal before the skin went on ([M346 Projects – Pt 14 (2024), 41:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=4_LqrbAlNmw&t=2500s)). Final filler and paint come in [Phase 13](#phase-13-paint-trim-and-paperwork).

**Check before you seal and prime:**

- The body diagonals match your figures from before welding.
- The lips sit at the marked height and offset, and match left to right.
- The tire clears both lips at full bump.
- The doors open, close and gap correctly.
- The inner lips are a sealed double skin.
- The filler neck reaches the M3 pot and seals.

### Rear doors

Decide with the door shut and aligned. Three treatments have been shown:

1. **Leave the door standard and finish the flare at the shut line.** This is how BMW built the prototype: BMW "decided against melding the rear wheel-arch sculpting into the door" ([Autocar](https://www.autocar.co.uk/car-news/features/best-car-bmw-never-built-driving-sublime-e46-m3-touring)). Petroyle finishes "the arch before the door exactly the same as the concept car" ([Hagerty – Petroyle's E46 M3 Touring (2026), 08:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=--YbwCS4F_g&t=520s)), and one e46fanatics builder kept flat doors "to follow the BMW prototype" ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-touring-project.1240087/)). Expect to trim or dress the door's trailing edge so it meets the new arch line cleanly, and to blend the flare's forward nose into the shut line.
2. **Overlay the flare on the door, then re-cut the door.** Tracks and Trails made a cardboard-and-tape template of the door edge, overlaid the front of the flare onto the door, then re-cut the gap from the template ([Tracks and Trails (2026), 15:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=940s), [21:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=nADBRfX9DqY&t=1300s)). Calvin's body man welded a section into the door so it "flicks out" to meet the flare ([Calvin's Car Diary – PT9 (2020), 02:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=140s)), and other builders have "added a bit of metal" to the door's trailing edge ([e46fanatics](https://www.e46fanatics.com/threads/m3-touring-project.1240087/)).
3. **Flare the door's trailing edge without welding.** SOS Customz pounded the edge out ([SOS Customz (2023), 02:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=L8m9gEOklVY&t=120s)). M346 marked start and stop points on the quarter, opened the door and massaged the edge out with a 2x4 ([M346 Projects – Pt 22 (2025), 00:40](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c6PDOLC94K0&t=40s)). HARD's rivet-on flare kit has separate rear-door pieces for the same reason.

**Restoring the gap.** Flaring the door after the quarter is on opens the shut gap by about 3–4 mm ([M346 Projects – Pt 22 (2025), 01:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c6PDOLC94K0&t=60s)). M346 built it back with 3M panel bond (38315) against a taped card wall about 4 mm thick, let it cure for 24 hours, sanded it with a hard block and checked it against the stock gap with feeler gauges ([04:00](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c6PDOLC94K0&t=240s), [22:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c6PDOLC94K0&t=1340s)). His reasoning: it's a small area that doesn't flex, where panel bond is stronger than filler ([07:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=c6PDOLC94K0&t=440s)).

**Trim that follows.** On a flared door, the straight side rubbing strip has to be reshaped to follow the flare, and one plastic side skirt was heated and bent out to meet it ([Calvin's Car Diary – PT9 (2020), 04:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=260s), [11:20](https://www.youtube.com/watch?v=-BMrhOr8fdg&t=680s)).

![BMW's E46 M3 Touring prototype, rear three-quarter view](photos/prototype-rear.jpg)

*Photo: BMW's prototype from behind. BMW kept the arch flare off the rear door skins ([Autocar](https://www.autocar.co.uk/car-news/features/best-car-bmw-never-built-driving-sublime-e46-m3-touring)). Alexander Migl, [CC BY-SA 4.0](https://creativecommons.org/licenses/by-sa/4.0/), via [Wikimedia Commons](https://commons.wikimedia.org/wiki/File:BMW_M3_%28E46%29_Touring_Retro_Classics_2025_DSC_7634.jpg).*

> [!TIP]
> A faster route is rivet-on flares. HARD Motorsport's M3-style rear set for the E46 sedan and Touring is ABS plastic with separate rear-door pieces, \$294 list (\$264.60 on sale in October 2026) ([HARD Motorsport](https://www.hardmotorsport.com/hard-motorsport-bmw-e46-sedan-touring-m3-style-motorsport-fender-flares-rear-set/)).
